## Document 003: 10月经济数据点评：以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动

证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

10 月经济数据点评

以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动   
10 月经济数据整体偏弱，表现较好的仅有高技术产业工业增加值同比增速；
我们认为国庆长假前后居民休假，可能对 10 月工作日时间的影响较明显，进
而导致经济数据表现偏弱。 
◼ 10 月工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，从行业分类来看，1-10 月采矿业工业增
加值累计同比增速 1.9%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用事业累计同比
增速 5.3%，高技术产业累计同比增速 14.6%。 
◼ 10 月社零同比增长 0.4%，其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长
2.5%，1-10 月服务消费累计同比增长 4.9%。 
◼ 1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速下降 8.1%，民间固定资产投资累计同
比下降 11.4%，扣除房地产开发投资，1-10 月全国固定资产投资下降 5.1%，
民间投资下降 7.8%。分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-10 月累计同比下降
2.9%，基建投资累计同比下降 7.3%，地产投资累计同比下降 21.6%。 
◼ 1-10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 13.7%，商品房销售额累计同比下降
14.3%。10 月，70 个大中城市新建商品住宅价格指数同比下降 2.7%，较
9 月上升 0.3 个百分点，二手住宅价格指数下降 4.4%，较 9 月上升 0.4 个
百分点。 
◼ 10 月经济数据偏弱。工业增加值同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且
低于万得一致预期，社零同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得
一致预期，1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速较 1-9 月下行 0.9 个百分点，
且低于万得一致预期。从细项看，10 月经济数据中表现较好的是工业增
加值中的高技术产业，以及依托外需的出口交货值，此前经济数据中表
现较好的部分，如固定资产投资中的设备购置累计同比增速、社零中的
通信工具当月同比增速，在 10 月也都出现了增速下降的情况。另一方面，
此前偏弱的数据尚未出现好转迹象，包括房地产固定资产投资增速和商
品房销售数据同比增速仍在下降，基建投资增速仍在下滑等等。考虑到
今年的国庆和中秋假期相邻，部分居民选择利用年假增加国庆前后出行
时间，我们认为 10 月经济数据偏弱，一定程度上可能有假期安排对工作
日天数缩短的影响。 
◼ 关注 2027 年经济增长目标设定。12 月政治局会议时间一般在上旬，这就
意味着 10 月经济数据对于定调 2027 年经济工作的影响较大。从 10 月数
据的特点来看，我们认为较为确定的几个方向：一是三驾马车中净出口
仍将是 2027 年经济增长的重要拉动力，二是消费偏弱的状态大概率将延
续，三是基建投资增速仍较大程度上依赖财政支出，短期内的亮点可能
是“六张网”，四是房地产行业即便有政策支持，整体或仍在寻底的过
程中，连带房地产产业链上下游中期内都将处于寻底过程，五是高技术
产业或将保持较好增速，六是国内通胀压力整体处于偏低水平，七是适
度宽松的货币政策环境和积极的财政政策大概率都将延续。我们认为
2027 年可能需要关注供给端的持续稳健经营：一方面，美国对 AI 科技发
展的监管政策前景存在不确定性，对于当前全球范围内 AI 带动的经济增
长结构或产生一定影响，进而影响我国外需，另一方面，全球范围内能
源价格上涨、国内 PPI 同比高增和人民币的升值趋势，都可能推升供给端
成本，进而影响企业生产经营。我们认为不排除 2027 年经济增长目标设
定为 2026 年增长目标区间的下限。 
◼ 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 2

工业增加值同比增速回落 
10 月工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，较去年同期下降 0.1 个百分点，低于万
得一致预期同比增速 5.32%。

图表 1. 工业增加值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券

从环比增速来看，10 月工业增加值环比上升 0.21%，与 2016 年-2019 年期间同期平均水平 0.47%相
比偏低,略低于去年同期的环比增速 0.22%，10 月工业增加值环比较 9 月下降,趋势上符合季节性规律，
但受 9 月环比基数偏高影响，10 月环比增速回落幅度较大。 
从行业分类来看，1-10 月采矿业工业增加值累计同比增速 1.9%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用
事业累计同比增速 5.3%，高技术产业累计同比增速 14.6%。从企业类型来看，1-10 月国企工业增加
值同比增速 3.6%，股份制企业同比增速 5.7%，外企工业增加值同比增速 3.2%，私企工业增加值同
比增速 4.2%。与 1-9 月相比，1-10 月累计同比增速上升的仅有高技术产业,下降的是国企、采矿业、
公共事业、股份制企业和私企。10 月当月工业增加值同比增速相较 9 月，各行业和企业类型中工业
增加值同比增速上升的也仅有高技术产业，下降幅度超过 1.0 个百分点的是国企和公共事业。 
1-10 月，装备制造业增加值同比增长 10.6%，高技术制造业增加值增长 17.6%，分别快于全部规模
以上工业增加值 5.8 和 12.8 个百分点；分产品看，3D 打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产品产量
同比分别增长 85.9%、30.0%、26.0%。

图表 2. 工业增加值环比增速  图表 3. 主要行业工业增加值当月同比增速

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 3

10 月，采矿业和大宗周期品加工业当月工业增加值同比增速偏弱，中游设备制造业工业增加值表现
相对较好。 
采矿业方面，1-10 月煤炭开采和洗选业增加值累计同比下降 0.1%，石油和天然气开采业增加值累计
同比增长 6.1%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速小幅上升的是煤炭开采，小幅下降的是油气开采，
但煤炭开采行业 10 月当月工业增加值同比仍在负增长区间。 
消费类制造业方面，1-10 月农副食品加工业增加值累计同比增长 4.5%，纺织业增加值累计同比增长
3.0%，医药制造业增加值累计同比增长 5.3%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速上升的是医药制造
业，降幅超过 1.0 个百分点的是纺织业。 
大宗周期品加工业方面，1-10 月化学原料及化学制品制造业增加值累计同比增长 3.3%，非金属矿物
制品业增加值累计同比下降 2.9%，黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比增长 1.4%，有色金属
冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比下降 1.9%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速上升的有色金属和
非金属矿物，增速升幅较小，但较 9 月增速下降的化学原料和黑色金属降幅均超过 1.0 个百分点。 
中游设备制造业方面，1-10 月通用设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 8.2%，专用设备制造业增加值累
计同比增长 9.7%，汽车制造业增加值累计同比增长 6.5%，电气机械及器材制造业增加值累计同比增
长 7.2%，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 16.2%；从当月同比增速来看，
较 9 月增速上升的仅有计算机通信，且升幅达到 3.0 个百分点，下降的是汽车制造业和专用设备制
造业，其中汽车制造业较 9 月降幅达到 5.8 个百分点，10 月当月工业增加值同比增速仅为 0.9%。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 4

10 月外需延续较强走势，出口交货值同比继续大幅上升，产销率较去年同期小幅上升。 
10 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.5%，较 9 月下降 0.5 个百分点，较去年同期上升 0.1 个百分点，
当月同比增速下降 0.4%；1-10 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.4%，较去年同期上升 0.4 个百分点，
累计同比增速持平。 
10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，较 9 月上升 1.6 个百分点，1-10 月规模以上工
业企业出口交货值累计同比增长 11.6%，较 1-9 月上升 0.9 个百分点。

图表 8. 产销率当月值和累计同比增速  图表 9. 出口交货值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

社零增速再度回落 
10 月社零同比增长 0.4%，较 9 月下降0.7 个百分点，其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长2.5%，
较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点；1-10 月社零累计同比增长 1.0%，其中，服务消费累计同比增长 4.9%，较
1-9 月下降 0.1 个百分点。 
10 月社零环比增速增长 0.02%，较去年同期环比增速下降 0.09 个百分点，较历史同期平均水平明显
偏低。

图表 10. 社会消费品零售额同比增速

### Page 5

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 5

图表 11. 汽车以外社零和服务消费增速  图表 12. 社零环比增速的历史波动

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

1-10 月网上商品和服务零售额累计同比增长 3.7%，增速较 1-9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，其中实物商品
网上零售额累计同比增长 2.9%，增速较 1-9 月下降 0.9 个百分点。10 月网上零售额累计占比为 40.8%，
较 2026 年同期上升 9.7 个百分点，较 1-9 月下降 0.2 个百分点。 
10 月商品消费同比增长 0.3%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，限额以上商品消费同比下降 5.1%，较 9
月下降 2.6 个百分点；10 月餐饮消费同比增长 0.7%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点，限额以上餐饮消费
同比下降 2.9%，较 9 月下降 4.9 个百分点。1-10 月，在服务零售额中，通讯信息服务类、旅游咨询
租赁服务类、文体休闲服务类零售额较快增长。 
1-10 月限额以上企业消费品零售总额累计同比下降 1.9%，限额以上企业商品消费累计同比下降 2.1%,
弱于餐饮消费累计同比增速 0.9%。

图表 13. 网上零售总额的占比和增速  图表 14. 限额以上企业社零增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

10 月受价格因素影响，石油制品消费增速有所上升，房地产后周期消费依然偏弱，国庆假期时间较
长，一定程度上拖累了居民消费增速表现。 
从产品分类来看，10 月限额以上企业商品零售总额增速较高的细分行业包括通讯器材类（6.5%）、
石油及制品类（4.4%）和文化办公用品类（4.1%），增速较低的细分行业包括金银珠宝类（-28.7%）、
汽车类（-21.4%）和建筑及装潢材料类（-11.2%）；与 9 月相比，增速上升的有石油及制品类、体
育娱乐用品类、家用电器和音像器材类和家具，增速下降较多的有通讯器材类、金银珠宝类和烟酒
类等。

图表 18. 固定资产投资环比增速的季节规律  图表 19. 固投各项构成分类的累计同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

10 月制造业、基建和房地产当月投资增速较 9 月均出现回落。 
从产业分类来看，第一产业累计同比下降 6.1%，第二产业累计同比下降 3.6%，第三产业累计同比下
降 10.9%；从地区来看，东部累计同比下降 11.5%，中部下降 12.9%，西部下降 9.2%，东北下降 25.9%；
从企业类型来看，内资企业累计同比下降 7.9%，国有控股同比下降 4.0%，外商投资下降 6.6%。 
分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-10 月累计同比下降 2.9%，基建投资累计同比下降 7.3%，地产投资累
计同比下降 21.6%。从我们测算的当月同比增速看，10 月制造业投资同比增速下降 9.3%，较 9 月下
降 8.4 个百分点，基建投资同比增速下降 17.4%，较 9 月下降 6.9 个百分点，房地产投资同比增速下
降 34.2%，较 9 月下降 7.0 个百分点。 
1-10 月固投增速在正增长区间的一级分类行业是采矿业（2.5%）和交运仓储（0.7%），固投增速较
低的行业包括文体娱（-18.5%）、教育（-15.7%）和水利环境（-14.4%）。制造业行业分类下，固
投增速较高的细分行业包括铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业（14.1%）、计算机、通信
和其他电子设备制造业（9.9%）和纺织业（2.8%），固投增速较低的行业包括食品制造业（-10.7%）、
金属制品业（-9.9%）和医药制造业（-9.2%）。 
与 1-9 月相比，一级分类行业中固投增速降幅较小的行业是卫生社会、教育和采矿业，固投增速下
降较多的行业是农林牧渔业、水利环境和文体娱。制造业行业分类下，固投增速上行的行业仅有计
算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业和金属制品业，固投增速下行较多
的行业是通用设备制造业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业和汽车制造业。 
据统计局解读，1-10 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，高技术产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信
息服务业，航空、航天器及设备制造业，电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.5%、10.2%、6.6%。

### Page 10

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 10

结论：以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动 
10 月经济数据偏弱。工业增加值同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期，社零同
比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期，1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速较 1-9
月下行 0.9 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期。从细项看，10 月经济数据中表现较好的是工业增加值
中的高技术产业，以及依托外需的出口交货值，此前经济数据中表现较好的部分，如固定资产投资
中的设备购置累计同比增速、社零中的通信工具当月同比增速，在 10 月也都出现了增速下降的情况。
另一方面，此前偏弱的数据尚未出现好转迹象，包括房地产固定资产投资增速和商品房销售数据同
比增速仍在下降，基建投资增速仍在下滑等等。考虑到今年的国庆和中秋假期相邻，部分居民选择
利用年假增加国庆前后出行时间，我们认为 10 月经济数据偏弱，一定程度上可能有假期安排对工作
日天数缩短的影响。 
关注 2027 年经济增长目标设定。12 月政治局会议时间一般在上旬，这就意味着 10 月经济数据对于
定调 2027 年经济工作的影响较大。从 10 月数据的特点来看，我们认为较为确定的几个方向：一是
三驾马车中净出口仍将是 2027 年经济增长的重要拉动力，二是消费偏弱的状态大概率将延续，三是
基建投资增速仍较大程度上依赖财政支出，短期内的亮点可能是“六张网”，四是房地产行业即便有
政策支持，整体或仍在寻底的过程中，连带房地产产业链上下游中期内都将处于寻底过程，五是高
技术产业或将保持较好增速，六是国内通胀压力整体处于偏低水平，七是适度宽松的货币政策环境
和积极的财政政策大概率都将延续。我们认为 2027 年可能需要关注供给端的持续稳健经营：一方面，
美国对 AI 科技发展的监管政策前景存在不确定性，对于当前全球范围内 AI 带动的经济增长结构或
产生一定影响，进而影响我国外需，另一方面，全球范围内能源价格上涨、国内 PPI 同比高增和人
民币的升值趋势，都可能推升供给端成本，进而影响企业生产经营。我们认为不排除 2027 年经济增
长目标设定为 2026 年增长目标区间的下限。 
风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

### Page 11

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 11

披露声明 
本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。 
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况。如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的，请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。 
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 008: 10月金融数据点评：需求从哪里来

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月金融数据点评：需求从哪里来.pdf
Report source: 中银证券
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济 | 证券研究报告 — 总量点评 2026 年 11 月 15 日

证券分析师：朱启兵

Qibing.Zhu@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

10 月金融数据点评

需求从哪里来   
10 月新增社融和新增信贷均低于万得一致预期，政府债融资对社融的贡献较
大，居民部门和企业部门融资需求都明显偏弱，新增信贷出现流向非银部门
的现象。 
 10 月新增社融 1.14 万亿元，较去年同期多增 3251 亿元，较 9 月少增 2.41 万亿元，
低于万得一致预期的 1.33 万亿元。10 月社融存量同比增长 7.2%，较 9 月上升了
0.07 个百分点，略低于万得一致预期的 7.27%。10 月社融口径新增人民币贷款少
增 5846 亿元，较去年同期少增 5692 亿元，较 9 月少增 1.86 万亿元。 
 10 月新增社融较去年同期多增，主要原因是政府债券较去年同期多增了 6983 亿
元，此外较去年同期多增的还有汇票、企业债、外币贷款和委托贷款；从当月新
增规模看，新增融资规模较大的项目，主要是政府债券 1.18 万亿元、新增企业债
融资 3446 亿元和委托贷款 1862 亿元，当月少增的是人民币贷款、汇票和信托贷
款。值得关注的是 10 月股票融资规模较 9 月明显下降，较去年同期也明显下降，
一定程度上反映出市场存在向下调整压力后，市场融资行为也出现了收紧。 
 从社融存量结构来看，2026 年 10 月与 9 月相比，占比上升的有政府债券（上升
0.20 个百分点）、企业债（上升 0.05 个百分点）和委托贷款（上升 0.04 个百分
点），占比下降的是人民币贷款（下降 0.25 个百分点）、汇票（下降 0.04 个百
分点）和信托贷款（下降 0.01 个百分点）。与去年同期相比，10 月占比上升较
多的是政府债券（上升 1.43 个百分点），占比下降较多的是人民币贷款（下降
1.47 个百分点）。低利率环境下，企业债融资规模占比持续上升。 
 10 月 M2 同比增长 7.1%，较 9 月下降 0.1 个百分点；M1 同比增长 4.4%，较 9 月
上升 1.2 个百分点；M0 同比增长 10.7%，较 9 月上升 0.1 个百分点。10 月活期存
款（M1-M0）环比上升 0.16%，定期存款（M2-M1）环比下降 0.29%，与去年同
期相比，活期存款环比增速较高，定期存款环比增速偏低。央行三季度货币政策
执行报告的专栏 1 中也提出，我国存款结构出现了诸如非银行业金融机构存款增
长较快、占比明显抬升，以及居民和企业存款呈现一定程度定期化的特征，但受
重要节假日、财政收支节奏等影响，居民、企业、财政存款会出现季节性波动，
因此应该更关注趋势性变化。 
 10 月关注非银存款多增。10 月金融机构新增存款 1.21 万亿元，多增的主要是非
银存款 1.58 万亿元和财政存款 1.40 万亿元，企业存款和居民存款均出现少增；
与去年同期相比，10 月新增存款多增 6000 亿元，其中财政存款多增 6800 亿元，
居民存款多增 4200 亿元。10 月财政存款多增规模较大，一方面是与政府债融资
规模较去年同期多增相对应，另一方面也与财政收支节奏有关，我们认为财政存
款多增为 2026 年四季度到 2027 年稳增长提供了资金保障；非银存款多增与居民
存款少增并存，我们认为虽然资本市场短期向下调整，但投资者对人民币股票市
场前景仍相对乐观。 
 10 月实体经济融资需求延续偏弱。10 月金融机构口径新增人民币贷款少增 1000
亿元，低于万得一致预期的 591 亿元。其中，新增短贷及票据少增 5717 亿元，
新增中长贷 37 亿元，企业贷少增 1600 亿元，居民贷少增 4300 亿元，多增幅度
较大的是金融同业贷 3470 亿元。我们认为 10 月信贷数据显示实体经济融资需求
依然偏弱，一方面是在较低的利率水平下，中长贷和企业债融资都对短贷构成挤
出，另一方面是全球经济前景存在不确定性，居民和企业扩张资产负债表的需求
明显下降。需要关注的是，金融同业贷规模明显上升，可能与证券、基金、保险
公司互换便利（SFISF）操作有关。 
 关注需求从哪里来。从三季度货币政策执行报告来看，未来较长一段时间之内适
度宽松的货币政策仍在，但我们认为 2026 年四季度至 2027 年最重要的问题是需
求从哪里来：从国内的角度看，居民新增贷款较去年同期少增的趋势延续，指向
居民消费和房地产销售可能持续疲弱，企业融资需求偏弱指向生产端扩张预期不
强，因此内需将延续依赖财政支出拉动的特点；从海外看，一方面主要央行仍在
加息通道当中，货币政策收紧可能导致需求下降，另一方面，美国中期选举后，
对 AI 的监管政策仍有一定变数，因此拉动全球经济上行重要动力的不确定性加
大。我们认为未来一段时间内，对金融数据的关注重点或不在于货币政策工具如
何调整，而在于实体经济金融环境保持平稳且适度宽松，建议重点关注利率水平
的波动。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济回落速度偏快；国际局势复杂化。

以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

## Document 033: 2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

160 円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない 
～164 円への円安も視野、介入警戒の磁力が一段安を阻むか～

調査部  主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ ドル円の安定はかりそめ 
9月下旬以降、1ドル＝150円台後半での小動きが続いたが、これをもって「相場が安定している」と
捉えるのは危うい。チャート上は、上値目途として意識される200日移動平均線が150円台半ばに位置
し、同水準へ向けた円安方向への動きが繰り返された（図表1）。このところの値動きの狭さは、円安
方向を根強く試す動きが抵抗線付近で抑えられた結果とみる方が実態に近そうだ。 
11月に入ってからは、円高になりうる要因が散見されたにもかかわらず、市場ではあまり材料視さ
れていない。米国中間選挙（11月3日）の投開票後には、米国のデータセンター建設投資の持続性に対
する懸念が強まったことで株価が大きく下落した（図表2）。震源地である米国から日本に資金を退避
する動きが生じれば円高要因になるが、ドル円の値動きは限定的だった。また、中東情勢に対する楽
観的な見方から原油安が進展しており、円需給の観点からは先行きの本邦輸入金額の減少を通じて円
安圧力を緩和する要因になるが、この点も現時点では円高につながっていない。 
足元では、米利上げ観測の再燃を背景に再び円安が進んでおり、円安圧力は一段と強まっている。
本稿執筆時点では、心理的節目の200日移動平均線を超え、159円台後半まで上昇している。 
図表1 ドル円レート 図表2 日米株価指数

ドル円

### Page 2

2

本稿では、円安圧力の背景にある要因を改めて整理したうえで、それらを考慮したドル円の推計値
を試算する。実勢値との比較を通じて、足元の円安水準が現在の経済環境に照らしてどの程度説明で
きるのか検証し、その結果をもとに先行きのドル円を展望する。 
■ 現時点でのドル円の適正水準は 1 ドル＝163.70 円 
1ドル＝160円の円安を試す動きは、現在の経済環境に裏付けられたものなのだろうか。この点を検
証するために、東深澤（2026）で構築したモデルを用いて、現在の経済環境から説明されるドル円の
水準を確認する（推計式の詳細や構築過程については東深澤（2026）を参照されたい）。このモデル
を用いて足元の経済環境と整合的なドル円を試算すると、推計値は1ドル＝163.70円と実勢値より円安
になる（図表3）。足元の経済環境下では、160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではないということだ。 
モデルが円安水準を支持している理由として最も大きいのが、日本の期待インフレ率の上昇だ。2022
年以降の円安を要因分解すると、米金利上昇や実需の円需給環境の悪化に加え、日本の期待インフレ
率の上昇が大きく寄与している（図表4）。物価が上がれば、同じ金額で買える財・サービスが少なく
なり、円の購買力は低下する。先行きも物価上昇が続くとの見方が強まれば（＝期待インフレ率の上
昇）、将来の円の価値が低下することを織り込む過程で、現在の円相場にも円安圧力がかかると考え
られる。 
実際、日本の期待インフレ率上昇と円安との関係は、データからも確認される。図表5をみると、日
米期待インフレ率格差が縮小すると、円安が進むという対応関係がうかがえる。米国の期待インフレ
率はおおむね安定しており1、日米期待インフレ率格差の縮小は、主に日本の期待インフレ率が上昇し
ていることによる。こうした期待インフレ率の高まりは、賃金・物価上昇の定着にともなう構造的な
変化2だ。期待インフレ率が反転して大幅に低下することは短期的に見込みづらいため、1ドル＝160円
程度の円安水準が定着することは自然だと言える。 
加えて、足元では米金利の上昇も円安圧力となっているようだ。11月以降は、市場予想を上回る物
図表3 ドル円の推計値と実勢値 図表4 2022年以降の円安要因

### Page 3

3

価指標や、ウォラー理事など複数のFRB高官発言がタカ派的と受け止められたことを背景に、米金利
は再度上昇に転じつつある。 
■ 今後は、介入警戒の磁力が一段の円安を阻むかが焦点に 
先行き、推計値（1ドル=163.70円）との乖離を解消する形で円安が進む可能性は十分にある。円安進
行のドライバーになりうるのが、投機筋に積み上がった円買いポジションの巻き戻しだ。図表6は、実
勢値と推計値の乖離を、投機筋の円ポジションに重ねたものだ。これをみると、円買いポジションが
拡大する局面では、実勢値が推計値より円高になる場面が多いことがわかる。とりわけ9月中旬にかけ
ては、日銀の追加利上げ観測などを背景に投機筋の円買い超過幅が拡大した。円買いポジション調整
の余地は十分にあり、巻き戻しの動きが顕在化する場合は1ドル＝164円近傍までの円安進行が視野に
入ってくるということだ。 
こうしたシナリオが実現するかどうかは、為替介入への警戒の持続性に左右される。7月末の日米協
調介入も記憶に新しく、今のところ為替市場は介入への警戒感を維持していると考えられる。11月12
日には三村財務官が円安をけん制する発言を行うなど、日本政府が再び介入姿勢を強めている点を踏
まえれば、160円を持続的に上回るような円安進行は回避されるという見方が穏当だ。 
ただし、リスクは円安方向に大きい。特に、協調介入に対する米国側の姿勢が反転するシナリオに
注意しておきたい。7月当時と比べると、米国政府にとって協調介入の必要性が低下している可能性が
あるためだ。米国政府は当時、日本の長期金利上昇と円安が米国債市場に波及し、米金利を押し上げ
る経路を意識していたとみられる。足元では日米ともに長期金利の上昇が一服しており、日本発の市
場の混乱を防ぐ動機が弱まっている可能性は相応にある。今後、米国政府高官の発言などを機に、米
国が協調介入実施に慎重という見方が広がれば、市場の介入警戒感が和らぎかねない。その場合は、
投機筋のポジション調整を伴う形で、160円を上回って円安が進行するリスクに注意が必要だ。 
以上を踏まえ、2026年末にかけては、米利上げ観測が続き、150円台後半～160円近辺での円安水準
が続く展開を予想する。ただし、投機筋の円買いが巻き戻されたり、協調介入への警戒が和らいだり

不確実性は円安リスクに」、みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ『みずほリポート』、2024年4
月23日

1 この間、米国の期待インフレ率は概ね 2%台半ばで横ばい推移となっている。2026 年の中東情勢悪化を受けて急速な原油高が
進展した場面でも、期待インフレ率の急激な上昇は回避されている。背景には、米国の利上げ期待が急速に進んだことにある。
FRB が迅速に利上げを進めることで、先行きのインフレ率は安定するだろうという市場の期待が反映されていると解釈すること
ができる。 
2 実需要因も、円相場の趨勢を考えるうえで重要な要因である。足元の相場変動への寄与は限定的なため本文では詳述していな
いが、実需面の構造変化に関する分析は東深澤ほか（2024）を参照されたい。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

GCE’s 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 6%/1% below GSe/BBG 
consensus due mainly to (1) key AI customer’s product transition 
period (revenue in 3Q was 4/3% lower than GSe/BBG consensus), 
and (2) the increasing investment in R&D to improve its 
production capability on AI HDI and N+M MLB products), despite 
the much stronger than expected GM (0.8/1.8ppt higher than 
GSe/BBG consensus). For the net income in 3Q26, it was 5% below 
our bullish estimate, resulting from the weaker than expected 
operating profit, but it was 3% higher than BBG consensus, thanks to 
the lower than expected tax rate resulting from the increasing profit 
contribution from the low tax rate Thailand plant (tax rate was only 
at 25% in 3Q26 vs. BBG consensus at ~30%).  
For 4Q26, we expect the company’s revenue to grow by 7% QoQ, 
due to ongoing key AI customers’ product transition, and the fewer 
working days due to the holiday season. We expect GCE’s GM will 
further expand to 39.2% (up 0.5ppt QoQ or 5.5ppt YoY) thanks to 
the better product mix (high-end networking demand should ramp 
up more significantly from 4Q26, per GSe), despite the increasing 
cost from high-end CCL (see here).  
Investment view - GCE will continue to deliver a better margin 
profile with expanding market share in AI ASIC market 
We continue to expect GCE will hold the major share for Trainium 3 
(70%+ from 3Q26), and see an even better market share in the next 
generation Trainium 4 products (as key competitors are all unable to 
support high-end AI HDI products) after the company ramps up its 
AI HDI production yield rate. We also expect the company to start 
supplying the new TPU v8 PCB products, which should meaningfully 
improve the company’s market share in coming quarters (from <5% 
in 2026 to 20-30%+ in 2027), suggesting an even better GM outlook 
in the foreseeable future.  
On the other hand, the company will only expand its capacity

### Page 3

volume by ~20% in 2027, without considering the product mix improvement/ASP hike 
(we believe after considering the better ASP for next-gen AI products, the company’s 
capacity value should go up by 35%+ YoY in 2027), while we believe the AI PCB 
volume/area demand should at least increase by 50% YoY, implying a potential market 
share loss in coming quarters. We note Greater China peers are also expanding capacity 
in SEA regions to try to penetrate into GCE’s key AI ASIC markets, implying increasing 
competition in coming quarters, which could lead to less favorable ASP in the long term, 
despite no significant impact for now. However, considering GCE’s best in class AI PCB 
production technology and its proactive investment in the high-end PCB R&D for more 
than 10 years, as well as the company’s first mover advantage in the SEA PCB market, we 
expect GCE will continue to have opportunities to expand its business in the most 
high-end PCB market, suggesting a good GM outlook in the long term.  
Maintain Buy on GCE, with a new 12-m TP of NT$1,660 (from NT$1,800).

Earning and TP revision

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026/27/28E net income estimate by 9%/6%/5% to factor in the 
potential slower pricing hike outlook in the PCB industry, considering the increasing 
competition in the AI PCB industry (cut 2026/27/28E revenue estimate by 7/6/5%). 
Moreover, we believe GCE will need to invest more in R&D projects in the coming 
quarters to improve its HDI production capability, and expect the N+M PCB R&D 
investment will also drive OPEX in coming years, considering the increasing adoption 
rate from 1H27 (revise up 2027 OPEX to sales ratio by 0.3ppt).

### Page 4

TP revision 
Maintain Buy with a new 12-m TP of NT$1,660 (from NT$1,800), based on 15x P/E 
(down from 20x previously, still in line with AI PCB peers’ average valuation); we also 
rollover our valuation base from 2027E to 2H27-1H28E, and factor in our earnings 
revision.

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

GCE is a key cloud (server+switch) PCB supplier globally with 20%+ market share in 
2022. We expect cloud PCB market demand to grow at a 15% 2024-26E CAGR (far 
stronger than the overall RPCB+HDI market demand growth rate at 5% 2024-26E 
CAGR). We believe GCE’s proactive capacity expansion plan (33% in 2026) and high 
annualized ROI (300%+) will continue to drive the company’s revenue/margin level in the 
coming quarters/years. Additionally, we believe GCE can continue to see strong demand 
from the increasing content per server/switch, as we expect both markets to see a 
20-50%+ layer count increase after the generation upgrade (upgrade to 400G/Eagle 
Stream). We are constructive on GCE’s long-term growth opportunity given its solid 
position in the rapidly growing cloud PCB market, and we are Buy rated on the stock. 
GCE is trading higher than its historical P/E average level driven by strong AI server 
demand, but lower than the TW AI server players’ average. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,660 is based on a 15x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with AI PCB peers average valuation).  
Key downside risks: (1) weaker-than-expected AI server market demand; (2) delays in 
the company’s Nvidia OAM/UBB product qualification, (3) weaker-than-expected HDI 
demand, and (4) higher-than-expected ramp up cost for new capacity

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 1

Better Interest Spread — Group interest spread widened 8bps qoq to 6.95%, 
mainly driven by better China spread. China spread improved 19bps qoq, as loan 
yield increased 8bps qoq and funding cost was 11bps lower qoq. Taiwan loan spread 
narrowed 7bps qoq, primarily due to 6bps qoq higher funding cost amid Fed rate 
hike and tightening domestic liquidity. 
Muted Loan Growth — Group loans grew by 1% qoq/4% yoy, mainly driven by 
ASEAN portfolio growth and RMB appreciation. Taiwan portfolio was flat qoq/-1% 
yoy. China portfolio was +2% qoq/flat yoy in local currency (+3% qoq/+11% yoy in 
NTD). Management suggested that the China business has achieved positive loan 
growth in the past three months and they expected positive momentum ahead. 
ASEAN portfolio was+2% qoq/+10% yoy,  
Broadly Stable China Asset Quality, Higher ASEAN Credit Cost — Group 
delinquency ratio slightly increased 3bps qoq to 5.16%. Taiwan delinquency ratio 
increased 20bps qoq, while NPL formation improved qoq. China delinquency ratio 
was stable qoq, but NPL formation slightly picked up qoq per our estimates. Group 
credit cost was 2.49%, +11bps qoq, mainly due to higher ASEAN credit costs on the 
back of macro uncertainties especially in Cambodia. 
Implications — Despite steady profit growth in 10M26 and stabilizing China asset 
quality, Chailease shares continued to de-rate this year amid concerns over growth. 
Management sounded more upbeat on China loan growth outlook at the results 
briefing, and the impact from auto financing business on Taiwan loan growth should 
gradually fade. FY26E dividend yield of 6% is attractive, in our view.

### Page 2

Chailease Holding (5871.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
2
Chailease Holding
Valuation
Our target price for Chailease of NT$130 is based on 1.2x 2026E P/B, using a Gordon Growth model. Our valuation
methodology takes into account a sustainable ROE estimate of 12.1%, a cost-of-equity assumption of 11.1%, and a long-term
growth rate of 6.0%. We use a blended approach to calculate both risk-free rates and long-term growth rates taking into
account local estimates on these parameters in the major markets in which Chailease operates, namely Taiwan, China, and

Thailand, and weighted them by asset mix.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the Chailease stock from reaching our target price are: [1] slower-than-expected asset
growth momentum; [2] asset quality deterioration in different markets; [3] higher operating expenses, which drag bottom-line
growth; [4] earlier-than-expected capital-raising that dilutes ROE and BVPS; and [5] ASEAN expansion that is slow in
contributing to group earnings. Key upsides risks to our target price include: [1] faster-than-expected asset quality recovery as

macro uncertainties ease; [2] higher-than-expected asset growth momentum.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV

1 
3167 大量(Ta Liang)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研 究 員：翁浩軒 (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 公司 TM4 背鑽機具技術領先優勢 
 成型機受惠光模塊建置需求放量成長 
 半導體及光通訊檢量測為新成長動能

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 10.02 元 
大量 3Q26 營收 36.71 億(QoQ+28.4%、YoY+159.5%)，受惠於 PCB
對高階鑽孔機需求持續放量，營收創歷史新高，毛利率升至 50.17%
優於預期，主要因高階 PCB 機台及 TM3 出貨提升，稅後淨利 9.05
億(QoQ+66.62%、YoY+320.97%)，EPS 10.02 元。

二、AI 帶動高階 PCB 設備放量 
受惠於 AI 趨勢，鑽孔機及成型機需求均放量，大量之鑽孔機接單
提升，高階機型 2025 年出貨 501 台，至 2026 年 10 月底已出貨 1189
台，全年出貨近 1500 台，2027 年已確定訂單已約 600 台，仍有許
多仍在洽談中，而內層銅量測 2025 年為 TM2/TM3 系列，出貨 19
台，2026 年 TM2/TM3 出貨 51 台，TM4 已出貨 35 台，至年底仍有
10 台訂單待出，2027 年預估將全年轉為 TM4，因終端客戶向 PCB
廠指定用大量的 TM4，我們預估有望出貨近 100 台。成型機部分，
今年前三季營收比重 30%，今年出貨比預期高，主要因光模塊需
求提升，採 mSAP 製程需用 CCD 成型機，公司成型機品質及市占
率高，預估 2027 年成長性不小於鑽孔機。整體而言，公司 PCB 在
手訂單已上千台，2027 年鑽孔機及成型機預期仍有雙位數成長。

三. 2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 31.34 元/ 65.01 元 
展望 2027 年，PCB 及半導體業務均大幅成長，AI 帶動高階 Server
板用量，PCB 鑽孔機、成型機需求強勁，高階 PCB 設備出貨維持
雙位數成長; 半導體業務方面，先進製程、先進封裝需求爆發，大
量跨入半導體領域營運逐步發酵，除了主流封裝外，CoWoS、
SoIC、FOPLP、CPO 產能擴增將帶動相關檢量測設備需求，預估大
量 2027 年半導體營收有望倍增。預估大量 2026 年營收 126.14 億
(YoY+148.41%)，毛利率 47.65%，稅後淨利 28.28 億(YoY+294.83%)，
EPS 31.34 元。預估 2027 年營收 221.39 億(YoY+75.71%)，毛利率
50.39%，稅後淨利 58.71 億(YoY+107.63%)，EPS 65.01 元。

        20.0 X 2027 EPS

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 1300 元 
大量為 PCB 鑽孔機、成型機大廠，為市場高階設備品質及技術領
導廠商，市占率亦高。過去設備出貨量隨景氣循環而波動明顯，
近年受惠於 AI 對高階 PCB 設備需求放量，加上切入半導體領域量
逐步發酵，公司檢量測設備於先進封裝之滲透率提升，加上 CPO
設備亦準備出貨，公司營運正向。考量到 1)高階 PCB 設備接單暢
旺、2)2027 年獲利翻倍成長、3)公司跨入先進封裝及 CPO 領域評
價有望提升，參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 20 倍本益比，目標價 1300 元，
建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
大量科技股份有限公司成立於 1980 年，總部位於桃園，公司以自
行研發製造控制器為競爭核心技術，發展鑽孔機、成型機等設備，
控制器可作為降低成型機振動及提升鑽孔精準度。公司業務含概
PCB 及半導體，PCB 設備產品包含 PCB 鑽孔定位、PCB 成型切割、
點膠、曲線滾塗等塗佈製程電漿之蝕刻、鍍膜等表面改質製程等；
半導體設備事業已於 2026 年 8 月 1 日分拆為大量先進智能，目前
由大量 100%持有，產品涵蓋 AOI、量測及自動化設備。公司之生
產工廠包括桃園八德廠、大陸南京廠、蘇州漣水廠，客戶含蓋兩
岸 PCB 廠及台灣 Foundry 及 OSAT 廠，包括欣興、健鼎、瀚宇博，
中國 PCB 廠深南、景旺、超聲等，台灣競爭對手包括台灣恩得、
東台、德國 SCHMOLL、中國大族激光等。

3 
二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 大量以內層銅深度量測建立背鑽技術門檻 
AI 算力需求對於資料傳輸要求提高，PCB 訊號傳輸加快下背鑽需
求提升，加上 PCB 層數增加對於背鑽精度要求亦提升。PCB 鑽孔
製程是經由通孔->填孔(通孔鍍銅)->背鑽，PCB 鑽孔將特定層數導
通後需要將多餘的銅柱鑽除，以降低訊號干擾，隨著 PCB 有效頻
寬從 Blackwell 28GHz 提升至 Rubin 56GHz，背鑽精度對殘銅要求
從±3mil(殘銅共振頻率約 45GHz)提升至±2mil(殘銅共振頻率
60GHz)，在背鑽機精度要求提高趨勢下，大量自製 TM4 量測機
可進行孔內深度量測，精準辨識孔內銅層/絕緣層實際位置，先建
立 3D 孔洞深度地圖後再進行背鑽，因此 TM4 量測機搭配大量自
製控制器與軟體的背鑽機成為高階 AI Server 背鑽的主流選擇，因
為 TM4 只與自有背鑽機整合銷售，不外搭外部機台，公司因此建
立背鑽技術門檻。

2. AI 帶動高階 PCB 設備放量 
大量主要產品以 PCB 設備為主，營收比重約 9 成，三大產品線包
含 PCB 鑽孔機、PCB 成型機及塗膠設備。鑽孔機涵蓋一般型與高
階型，差異在於是否有 CCD，另提背鑽量測設備(Stub Mapper) TM
系列，能將孔位精準量測，規劃背鑽深度地圖，精度可達正負 2mil; 
成型機涵蓋一般型與 CCD 高階成型機; 塗膠設備主要應用於
AI/AR 眼鏡邊緣及玻璃基板邊緣塗膠保護。鑽孔機部分，今年前三
季營收比重 62%，受惠於 AI 趨勢，Server 板均需要背鑽，大量之
鑽孔機接單提升，高階機型 2025 年出貨 501 台，至 2026 年 10 月
底已出貨 1189 台，全年出貨近 1500 台，2027 年已確定訂單已約
600 台，仍有許多仍在洽談中，而內層銅量測 2025 年為 TM2/TM3
系列，出貨 19 台，2026 年 TM2/TM3 出貨 51 台，TM4 已出貨 35
台，至年底仍有 10 台訂單待出，2027 年預估將全年轉為 TM4，因
終端客戶向 PCB 廠指定用大量的 TM4，我們預估有望出貨近 100
台。TM2/TM3 是單軸架構，適用前期樣品打樣；打樣獲得終端認
可、要進入批量量產時，必須採用四軸的 TM4 才能符合產能與
精度效率。成型機部分，今年前三季營收比重 30%，今年出貨比
預期高，主要因光模塊需求提升，採 mSAP 製程，需用 CCD 成型
機，公司成型機品質及市占率都高，預估 2027 年成長性不小於鑽
孔機。整體而言，公司 PCB 在手訂單台數已上千台，2027 年鑽孔
機及成型機預期仍有雙位數成長。

圖二、大量高階機種出貨

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

資料來源：大量

3. 半導體檢量測設備為新成長動能 
大量近年切入半導體領域，以大量先進智能(TLSmart)品牌行銷，
公司四大核心能力包括機構設計、快速設備控制、高精度量測與 
AI 檢測視覺系統、系統整合能力，產品線含蓋半導體前中後段，
主要提供 Metrology（量測）、AOI 檢測、自動化設備，公司在先
進封裝技術節點角色，包括 CoWoS：提供 CoW 及 oS 端各關鍵
站點的量測與檢驗設備; 3DIC / SoIC：提供三大量測系統（CMP Pad 
Metrology、Wafer Trim Gap Metrology、Wafer Bump Shift Edge Profile 
Metrology）; 面板級封裝（FOPLP / CoPoS）：因應 Chip 尺寸擴
大中介層需求轉向 Panel，推出支援 310×310mm、600×600mm、510
×515mm 之量測檢測設備，多數已達量產或小批量階段; 共同光學
封裝（CPO / 矽光子）：與美系國際 Tier 1 大廠共同開發 CPO OE 
與 Package 前處理系統、光學檢查機及後製程一體整合機，2026
年建第一條試產線，2027 下半年預估量產。半導體整體 BB 
Ratio>2，預估今年第四季出貨開始放量增加。

4. 今明年產能持續擴充 
公司目前自有產能已可達到 400 台/月，明年規劃提升至 500 台/
月以上，目前設備組裝時間方面，一般機型組裝約在 1 週以內，
高階機型、TM4 則約需 1.5 倍時間。公司除了自有工廠產能外，
亦接洽多家外包，2027 年如果外包認證順利產能有望達 600 台/月。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 10.02 元 
大量 3Q26 營收 36.71 億(QoQ+28.4%、YoY+159.5%)，營收創歷史
新高，毛利率升至 50.17%優於預期，主要因高階 PCB 機台及 TM3
出貨提升，稅後淨利 9.05 億(QoQ+66.62%、YoY+320.97%)，EPS 10.02
元。

2、2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 31.34 元/ 65.01 元 
預估大量 2026 年營收 126.14 億(YoY+148.41%)，毛利率 47.65%，
稅後淨利 28.28 億(YoY+294.83%)，EPS 31.34 元。預估 2027 年營收
221.39 億 (YoY+75.71%) ， 毛 利 率 50.39% ， 稅 後 淨 利 58.71 億
(YoY+107.63%)，EPS 65.01 元。

3Q26 Review: EV 판매 증가의 역풍 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 160,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 95,600 원(10/30)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1%yoy), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%yoy, OPM 5.8%), 지배이익 1.29조원(-9.8%yoy)을 기록, 도매 판
매 증가로 인한 매출 증가에도 불구하고 인센티브/원자재 비용 증가로 인해 
컨센서스를 하회하는 실적 쇼크를 기록 
 영업이익 증감 요인: 인센티브&가격 -7,270억원, 믹스 -4,650억원, 재료비 
-3,680억원으로 총 1.56조원 비용 발생. 증가 요인은 판매량 +4,420억원, 
환율 +5,290억원, 기타비용 절감 +4,130억원으로 1.38조원 상쇄. 인센티
브로 인한 비용 증가 예상외로 컸는데 EV 판매량 증가 영향. 기말환율 하락
으로 판매보증충당금은 전년비 9,254억원 감소 
 전동화 믹스 증가: 글로벌 xEV 소매 판매 30만대(+47%yoy), 판매비중 
36.2%(+9.8%p yoy). HEV 17.2만대(+46%yoy), 텔루라이드, 스포티지 
HEV 공급 확대로 3분기 미국 HEV 판매 +118%yoy 기록. EV 12.3만대
(+75%yoy), 서유럽 EV 판매는 +163%yoy 기록 
 4분기 전망: 중동 분쟁 지속으로 원가 부담의 급격한 개선은 어려울 전망. 
연초 CID에서 제시한 연간 판매 목표는 달성 가능할 것으로 보이지만 기존 
수익성 목표를 하회할 전망. 중장기적으로 상품 경쟁력 강화, 지역별 파워트
레인 대응 전략 유지, 내부 원가 및 체질 개선으로 외부 비용 부담 상쇄를 
추진할 계획. 중동 분쟁 완화에 따른 비용 부담 해소와 HEV 비중 확대를 통
한 수익성 개선은 2027년 시점이 될 것으로 예상 
 단기 방향 전환 어려울 듯: 판매 호조에도 불구하고 저가형 EV 판매 비중 
증가함에 따라 수익성이 악화되는 모습이 3분기에도 재확인. 글로벌 수요 
변화를 감안했을 때, EV 시장 선점이 매우 중요한 것은 사실이지만 단기 수
익성 부담 커지고 있는 점 우려됨. 중국산 EV의 글로벌 확산, 중동 전쟁 장
기화에 따른 원가 부담 증대가 연말까지는 영향을 미칠 것. 동사에 대한 투
자의견 매수와 목표주가 16만원 유지함

당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0%

## Document 263: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000 원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000 원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “September CPI Preview“.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,

Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%
which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The

distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “September CPI Preview“.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

## Document 288: Australian dwelling approvals_ units lift the headline (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian dwelling approvals_ units lift the headline (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
APPROVALS BULLETIN
Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

## Document 357: GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026年 11月 9日

1/3 
GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし 
～2026年 9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. 2026年 7～9月の収益率はマイナス 1.65％ 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）は 2026年 11月6日、同年 7～9月の運用実績を公表
した。期間収益率はマイナス 1.65％だった。7～9月は国内外の長期金利が上昇し、債券価格が下
落した。加えて円高も進み、外国資産の円換算額を押し下げた。資産別の収益率は国内債券がマ
イナス 1.80％、外国債券がマイナス 6.52％、国内株式がプラス 3.71％、外国株式がマイナス
1.86％となった。9月末の運用資産額は314兆3,876億円と 6月末から約 3.4兆円減少した。 
GPIFを巡っては、片山さつき財務・金融相が 7月に「日本の金融資産にさらなる投資をしてい
ただくという方向で後押しをする方策を追求したい」と発言し、ポートフォリオに変化があるか
注目が集まっていた。長期金利が上昇していたこともあり、特に国内債への関心が高かった。 
結論から言うと、国内債比率は基本ポートフォリオの 25％に極めて近く、運用姿勢の変化を読
み取ることは難しい。国内債の比率を引き上げるのではないかという観測は一旦後退するだろ
う。

2. 国内債券比率は 25％近辺を維持  
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは国内外の株式と債券をそれぞれ 25％とし、国内債券には±6ポイ
ントの乖離許容幅が設定されている。GPIFの公表資料によると、年金積立金全体に占める国内債
比率は 2026年9月末時点で 26.49％だった。これには年金特別会計で管理する積立金、約 5.4兆
円が含まれている。 
この積立金を国内債券と年金積立金全体から除き、GPIFの運用資産ベースで計算すると、9月末
の国内債券比率は25.23％となる。6月末の 24.98％から 0.25ポイント上昇したが、基本ポートフ
ォリオの25％をわずかに上回る水準だ（資料1）。 過去の実績と比較しても、今回の比率は突出し
ていない。2020～2025年度の 9月末平均は 25.05％で、2026年9月末はこれを0.18ポイント上回
る程度だ。

### Page 2

2/3 
資料 1 GPIFの運用資産構成比率（推計、2026年 6月末・9月末）

（注）年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いたGPIF運用資産ベース。 
（出所）GPIF「2026年度第 1四半期運用状況（速報）」、「同第 2四半期運用状況（速報）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成。

3. 国内債券の買越額は推計 1.4兆円  
次に、7～9月の国内債券への買越額を推計する。6月末と 9月末の国内債券残高の変化から、
期間中の運用損益による増減を除いて推計した。 
年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いた国内債券残高は、6月末の 79兆3,840億円から 9月末
の79兆3,206億円へ、634億円減少した。一方、7～9月の国内債券の運用損益はマイナス 1兆
4,452億円だった。この差額から、国内債券への買越額は約 1.4兆円と推計される（注 1）。4～6
月の約 5.7兆円と比較すると、国内債券への資金配分額は減少した（注2）。 
同じ方法で他の資産への資金配分を試算すると、外国債券は約 3.3兆円の買い越しとなった。
一方、国内株式は約2.0兆円、外国株式は約 0.8兆円の売り越しとなった。

4. 国内債券の運用姿勢に変化はみられず  
2026年7～9月の運用実績をみる限り、GPIFが国内債券の乖離許容幅を積極的に活用し、基本ポ
ートフォリオの25％を上回る水準へと比率を引き上げようとする動きは確認できなかった。 
国内債券は買い越しているとみられるものの、外国債券には国内債券を上回る資金が配分され
ている。4資産の比率はいずれも25％近い。基本ポートフォリオに近づけて運用する従来の姿勢
が維持されていると考えられる。 
以 上

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
【注釈】
1) 買越額は国内債券残高の変化から運用損益を控除した推計値。国内債券には為替ヘッジ付き外
国債券や円建て短期資産が含まれるため、日本国債の純購入額とは必ずしも一致しない。
2) 2026年7～9月は、GPIFの運用資産額の増減から運用収益額を差し引くと、約 1.9兆円の資金
流入があった計算になる。4～6月はほぼゼロだった。こうした資金流入の違いも、国内債券の
買越額を比較する際には考慮する必要がある。

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid-
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk, curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforces our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place: remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6-
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications: Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector: Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector. As we enter mid-cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress, S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3%, but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment-
grade corporates, offer attractive income. The 3-7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply: Since states 
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply-demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data-centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower-quality issuers. High-yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC-rated debt has reached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation, fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout, 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near-term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7. A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa. A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid-
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid-term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a defensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities, with clean energy stocks, including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters (see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook, 
the transition to mid-cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and AxJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive
